```text
ROLE
You are implementing PHASE 0 of a project called ThreatWeave — Agentic Multimodal

SCOPE OF THIS PHASE ONLY
Set up the repository skeleton and tooling. DO NOT implement any agent logic,
API endpoints, database models, or UI components yet — those are later phases.

TASKS
1. Create this exact folder structure (empty dirs get a `.gitkeep`):
   threatweave/
   ├── apps/web/                (Next.js app — do not scaffold yet, just the folder)
   ├── apps/api/app/{core,api/v1,models,schemas,services,agents,rag,db}/
   ├── apps/api/tests/
   ├── infra/{docker,nginx,github-actions}/
   ├── n8n/
   ├── docs/
   ├── .env.example
   └── docker-compose.yml (placeholder services only: postgres, chromadb — no app
       services yet, since apps/web and apps/api aren't built yet)

2. Initialize apps/api as a Python project:
   - pyproject.toml (or requirements.txt) listing: fastapi, uvicorn, sqlalchemy,
     alembic, pydantic, python-dotenv, langchain, langgraph, chromadb,
     psycopg2-binary, pytest
   - A minimal apps/api/app/main.py with a FastAPI instance and a single
     GET /health endpoint returning {"status": "ok", "service": "threatweave-api"}
   - apps/api/app/core/config.py using pydantic-settings, reading all secrets
     (DATABASE_URL, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, CHROMA_PATH, etc.) from environment
     variables only — NEVER hardcode any secret or default credential
   - apps/api/app/core/logging.py with basic structured (JSON) logging setup

3. Write .env.example listing every environment variable referenced in config.py,
   with placeholder values only (no real keys).

4. Write docker-compose.yml with `postgres:16` and `chromadb/chroma` services only,
   using environment variables (not hardcoded passwords) sourced from .env.

5. Write a root README.md explaining: project name, one-paragraph description
   matching "Agentic Multimodal Cyber Threat Intelligence & Response," the
   six-agent architecture (Orchestrator + Text + URL + QR + Image + Voice),
   the tech stack, and how to run `docker-compose up` plus the API health check.

6. Write a root .gitignore appropriate for a Python + Node monorepo
   (venv, node_modules, .env, __pycache__, .next, etc.).

7. Set up basic CI placeholder: infra/github-actions/ci.yml that on push runs
   `pip install` and a lint check (ruff or flake8) for apps/api — do not add
   deployment steps yet.

QUALITY REQUIREMENTS
- No hardcoded secrets anywhere, including docker-compose.yml.
- Every created file must be syntactically valid (valid TOML/YAML/Python/Markdown).
- Use clear inline comments in config.py explaining each setting.

VERIFICATION (run these and report exact output)
1. `cd apps/api && pip install -r requirements.txt` (or poetry/uv equivalent) —
   report success/failure.
2. `cd apps/api && uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8000 &` then
   `curl http://localhost:8000/health` — must return
   `{"status":"ok","service":"threatweave-api"}`. Report the exact response.
3. `docker-compose config` — must parse without error. Report the output.
4. Run any configured lint command and report output.

REPORT BACK TO ME, EXPLICITLY
- The full final folder tree that was created.
- Contents of .env.example.
- Exact output of each verification step above (paste real terminal output,
```

```env
# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# ThreatWeave Environment Variables Config (Example Template)
# Copy this file to '.env' at the root of the project and configure the values.
# Do NOT commit the '.env' file containing real secrets to version control.
# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENVIRONMENT=development
PORT=8000

# PostgreSQL Database Configuration
POSTGRES_USER=threatweave_user
POSTGRES_PASSWORD=your_secure_postgres_password_here
POSTGRES_DB=threatweave_db
POSTGRES_PORT=5432

# Database Connection URL (used by SQLAlchemy/Alembic in apps/api)

# Vector Database (ChromaDB) Configuration
CHROMA_PORT=8001
CHROMA_PATH=./chroma_db

# Optional Agent Extensions

TAVILY_API_KEY=your_tavily_api_key_here
```

### 5. Ambiguities Resolved
1. **Root Directory Placement**: The prompt specified `threatweave/` as the parent of folders `apps/`, `infra/`, etc. Since the workspace name is `d:\ThreatWeave — Agentic Multimodal`, I created a subdirectory `threatweave/` to hold all resources rather than polluting the workspace root.
2. **Uvicorn vs. ChromaDB Host Ports**: The FastAPI application is configured to run on host port `8000` to satisfy the verification requirement (`http://localhost:8000/health`). However, ChromaDB defaults to port `8000` as well. To prevent port conflicts on the host system during local deployment, ChromaDB's external port was mapped to `8001` (`8001:8000`) in `docker-compose.yml`.
3. **Import Packaging**: Initialized empty `__init__.py` files across all newly generated subfolders under `apps/api/app/` to ensure they behave correctly as importable Python packages.
4. **Ruff Code Styling**: Ruff initially flagged a few import grouping, type hinting, and deprecated time format (`datetime.utcnow()`) issues. I ran automatic fixes and converted the log formatter to use the modern, timezone-aware `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` method to resolve the errors.

```text
ROLE
You are implementing PHASE 1 of ThreatWeave (Agentic Multimodal Cyber Threat
Intelligence & Response). Phase 0 already exists at the repo root (threatweave/) —
inspect it first: read README.md and the existing folder structure before creating
anything. Do not touch apps/api/ in this phase. Do not overwrite any Phase 0 file.

SCOPE OF THIS PHASE ONLY
Build the Next.js frontend foundation and a reusable design-system layer only.
NO API integration, NO investigation workspace, NO upload UI, NO authentication.
Those are later phases — if you're tempted to build them now, stop and leave a
TODO comment instead.

DESIGN DIRECTION
Dark-first, professional SOC/threat-intelligence aesthetic — think a security
operations console, not a marketing landing page. Avoid: gradients, neon glow,
decorative accent stripes/borders, cream/beige backgrounds, generic AI-landing-page
look. Use: a near-black base (~#0B0E14–#10141C range), one restrained accent color
reserved only for primary actions/focus states, and a strict 5-tier severity system
(info/low/medium/high/critical) — each tier gets ONE fixed color + ONE fixed icon,
used identically everywhere it appears. Pick real hex values and a real type pairing
(display + body + a monospace face for indicators/IDs) — commit to specific choices,
don't leave defaults.

TASKS
1. Scaffold `apps/web` as a Next.js (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind) app —
   check if `apps/web` already has a `.gitkeep` only; if so it's empty, safe to
   scaffold into it directly.
2. Create `apps/web/lib/tokens.ts` exporting the full token system: colors
   (background scale, accent, 5 severity colors), spacing scale, type scale,
   radii. Every color used anywhere in the app must trace back to this file —
   no ad hoc hex codes in components.
3. Build these components in `apps/web/components/ui/`, each typed and reusable:
   - `Button` (primary/secondary/ghost variants)
   - `Card`
   - `Badge`
   - `SeverityTag` (props: severity: "info"|"low"|"medium"|"high"|"critical")
   - `StatusDot` (props: status: "idle"|"running"|"done"|"failed")
   - `Skeleton` (loading placeholder)
   - `EmptyState` (props: title, description, optional action — written in the
     interface's voice, e.g. "No investigations yet" + a clear next action,
     not a vague "Nothing here")
4. Build a placeholder dashboard at `apps/web/app/page.tsx` that composes these
   primitives: a page header, an empty investigation history table using
   `EmptyState`, and a small showcase row demonstrating all 5 `SeverityTag`
   states and all 4 `StatusDot` states, so the token system is visibly
   verifiable in this phase even with no real data.
5. Add keyboard focus states (visible outline using the accent color) on every
   interactive primitive. Respect `prefers-reduced-motion` for any transitions.
6. Write `apps/web/docs/design-system.md` documenting every token (with hex
   values) and one-line usage guidance for each component.
7. Add a "Running the frontend" section to the root README.md
   (`cd apps/web && npm install && npm run dev`).
8. Add `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8000` to a new
   `apps/web/.env.example` (unused this phase, wired up in a later phase).

QUALITY REQUIREMENTS
- TypeScript strict mode. No `any` unless justified with a comment.
- No hardcoded colors outside lib/tokens.ts.
- Components must be responsive down to 375px width.
- Run ESLint/Next lint and fix all reported issues before reporting done.

VERIFICATION (run these and report exact output)

2. `npm run build` — must succeed. Paste the output.
```

### 6. Ambiguities Resolved
1.  **Tailwind CSS Version Config**: Next.js v15+ scaffolding initializes **Tailwind CSS v4** by default. In Tailwind v4, the legacy `tailwind.config.ts` file is deprecated in favor of a CSS-first configuration model. I resolved this by placing all theme values directly inside `@theme` blocks in [`apps/web/app/globals.css`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/web/app/globals.css) and keeping `lib/tokens.ts` aligned in TypeScript.
2.  **Lucide Icon Definitions**: Mapped Lucide icons directly to the severity configurations inside [`SeverityTag.tsx`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/web/components/ui/SeverityTag.tsx) to ensure absolute consistency across the application.
3.  **TSLint Empty Interface**: The initial implementation of `SkeletonProps` defined an empty interface extending standard React HTML props. This triggered a typescript-eslint rule violation. I resolved this by converting it into a clean TypeScript type alias.

```text
SCOPE OF THIS PHASE ONLY
Restructure the FastAPI backend into versioned routing with centralized error
handling and middleware. NO database, NO file uploads, NO agent logic, NO auth —

TASKS
1. Create app/core/errors.py:
   - Base `ThreatWeaveError(Exception)` with `code: str`, `message: str`,
     `status_code: int`.
   - Subclasses: `NotFoundError` (404), `ValidationError` (422),
     `ExternalServiceError` (502) — this last one anticipates later phases
     where an LLM/reputation/STT API call fails and must degrade gracefully
     rather than crash.
   - A FastAPI exception handler registered in main.py that catches
     `ThreatWeaveError` and any unhandled `Exception`, returning a consistent
     JSON envelope: {"error": {"code": str, "message": str, "details": any}}.
     Unhandled exceptions must NEVER leak a raw Python traceback to the client —
     log the full traceback server-side via the existing structured logger, but
     return a generic "internal_error" code to the client.

2. Create app/core/middleware.py:
   - Request-ID middleware: generate a UUID per request, attach to
     request.state, include it in the JSON logs for that request, and return
     it in an `X-Request-ID` response header.
   - Timing middleware: log request duration in ms.
   - CORS middleware: allow only the origin from a new `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`
     setting (default "http://localhost:3000") — do NOT use "*".

3. Create app/api/v1/endpoints/health.py with a GET /health route returning
   {"status": "ok", "service": "threatweave-api", "version": "v1"}.
   Create app/api/v1/router.py that includes this endpoint and will later
   include other v1 routers (leave a comment showing where Phase 3+ routers
   will be added).

4. Update app/main.py to:
   - Register the error handlers and middleware from steps 1–2.

     that returns the same body (do not delete it — some tooling may already
     depend on it).

QUALITY REQUIREMENTS
```

### 5. Ambiguities Resolved
1.  **TestClient Exception Propagation**: By default, Starlette's `TestClient` propagates exception events out to the test runner instead of converting them to HTTP responses, causing tests verifying 500 error handlers to crash. I resolved this by instantiating the test client with `raise_server_exceptions=False` to let the validation suite assert our clean generic JSON envelopes.
2.  **CORS & Telemetry Middleware Ordering**: FastAPI evaluates middlewares in reverse order of addition. I registered `CORSMiddleware` before `RequestTelemetryMiddleware` to ensure CORS preflights are processed inside the telemetry layer (assigning them Request IDs and tracking execution times).
3.  **Pydantic Validator Class Name**: Pydantic v2 replaces `before_validator` with `BeforeValidator` when using it in type annotations via `Annotated`. I updated `config.py` to use `BeforeValidator` to prevent import and compilation issues during collection.

### 3. Verification of Model Relationships
All models define explicit `relationship()` mapping attributes (supporting lazy loading/compilation) rather than raw foreign key columns:
*   **[`user.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/models/user.py)**:
    *   `User.investigations`: `Mapped[list[Investigation]] = relationship("Investigation", back_populates="user")`
*   **[`investigation.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/models/investigation.py)**:
    *   `Investigation.user`: `Mapped[User] = relationship("User", back_populates="investigations")`
    *   `Investigation.evidence_inputs`: `Mapped[list[EvidenceInput]] = relationship("EvidenceInput", back_populates="investigation", cascade="all, delete-orphan")`
    *   `Investigation.agent_runs`: `Mapped[list[AgentRun]] = relationship("AgentRun", back_populates="investigation", cascade="all, delete-orphan")`
    *   `Investigation.conflict_logs`: `Mapped[list[ConflictLog]] = relationship("ConflictLog", back_populates="investigation", cascade="all, delete-orphan")`
    *   `Investigation.investigation_reports`: `Mapped[list[InvestigationReport]] = relationship("InvestigationReport", back_populates="investigation", cascade="all, delete-orphan")`
    *   `Investigation.rag_citations`: `Mapped[list[RagCitation]] = relationship("RagCitation", back_populates="investigation", cascade="all, delete-orphan")`
    *   `Investigation.risk_breakdown`: `Mapped[list[RiskBreakdown]] = relationship("RiskBreakdown", back_populates="investigation", cascade="all, delete-orphan")`

### 3. Decisions & Ambiguities Resolved
*   **Foreign Key Constraint Handling**: The `Investigation` model requires a non-nullable `user_id` to link to a valid User. Since authentication is a later phase, `ingest_investigation` dynamically checks for a default system analyst user record (`system_analyst@threatweave.local`) and creates it if missing, keeping the database schemas clean without compromising security keys.
*   **Un-imported Class Mapping Registry**: Standard FastAPI startup does not load all model files automatically. Added an import of [`base.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/db/base.py) directly inside [`main.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/main.py) so that uvicorn initializes all tables and mapping relationships immediately at boot.
*   **B008 Unused noqa**: Removed Form default noqa tags that did not trigger any B008 warnings to keep lint annotations clean.

```json

```

Running the request with the real `curl.exe` binary and adding an `Accept: application/json` header ensures the body is sent correctly.

```powershell
  http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/v1/debug/text-agent
```

Create app/core/url_heuristics.py with pure, network-free, individually testable functions: - has_https(url) -> bool - is_ip_address_host(url) -> bool (detect raw IP instead of domain name) - count_subdomains(url) -> int - is_known_shortener(url) -> bool (small curated list: bit.ly, tinyurl.com, t.co, goo.gl, is.gd, etc.) - has_suspicious_tld(url) -> bool (small curated weak-signal list, e.g. .tk, .ml, .ga, .xyz — document that this is a WEAK signal, never sufficient alone) - lookalike_score(url) -> float (simple edit-distance/similarity check against a small curated list of commonly-impersonated brand domains relevant to Indian banking/UPI/govt context — e.g. sbi, hdfcbank, icici, paytm, upi, incometax.gov.in-style patterns — return a 0-1 similarity score against the closest match) - run_heuristics(url) -> dict: combines all of the above into {"indicators": [...], "suggested_severity": ..., "details": {...}}. Require AT LEAST 2 corroborating flags before suggesting "high" or "critical" — a single weak signal like suspicious_tld alone must not reach high severity on its own. All functions must wrap URL parsing in try/except and handle malformed input gracefully (return False/0/safe defaults, never raise from a heuristic function). 3. Create app/agents/reputation_client.py: a function `check_reputation(url, timeout) -> dict | None` that calls an external URL/domain reputation API ONLY IF settings.URL_REPUTATION_API_KEY is set and non-empty — if unset, return None immediately without attempting any network call (this is a "not configured" case, log it at debug level, do not treat as an error). If configured but the call fails/times out, raise ExternalServiceError (reuse the existing pattern from llm_client.py) so the caller can catch it and continue without reputation data — never let a reputation API failure break the whole agent. 4. Create app/agents/prompts/url_agent_prompt.py: a system prompt that receives the ALREADY-COMPUTED heuristic findings (not the raw URL alone) and asks the model only to synthesize them into a coherent finding/ reasoning in plain language — explicitly instruct it NOT to invent new indicators beyond what's provided, and include the same "this is a sample for security analysis, not a live request" framing used in Phase 5's fixed text_agent_prompt.py. 5. #### Assistant Response

### Root Cause Diagnosis

The absence of `lookalike_domain` for `paypa1-verify.tk` came down to how `lookalike_score` previously compared domains:

1. **Full-string comparison**: The registered domain `paypa1-verify.tk` (17 chars) was directly compared against the full brand string `paypal.com` (10 chars).
2. **Dilution from TLD + combosquatting token**: Between the `.tk` vs `.com` mismatch, the hyphenated `-verify` suffix (+7 chars), and the `1` vs `l` substitution, the Levenshtein distance was **11**.
3. **Score fell below threshold**:
   $$\text{score} = 1.0 - \frac{11}{16} = 0.3125$$
   Because `0.3125 < 0.75`, `lookalike_domain` did not fire.

---

### Fix Implemented

In [`app/core/url_heuristics.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/core/url_heuristics.py):

1. **Stem Extraction (`_domain_stem`)**: Strips TLD/ccTLD to isolate the core domain label (e.g., `paypa1-verify.tk` $\to$ `paypa1-verify`, `sbi.co.in` $\to$ `sbi`, `paypal.com` $\to$ `paypal`).
2. **Combosquatting Tokenization**: Splits hyphen/underscore-delimited labels into sub-tokens (`paypa1-verify` $\to$ `["paypa1", "verify"]`).
3. **Homoglyph / Leetspeak Normalization (`_HOMOGLYPHS`)**: Maps character substitutions commonly used in phishing (`1` $\to$ `l`, `0` $\to$ `o`, `4` $\to$ `a`, `5` $\to$ `s`, etc.).
4. **Multi-level Comparison**: Compares the full registered domain, the domain stem, and each sub-token against brand stems. Non-exact matches are capped at `0.95` so that exact legitimate brands retain `1.0` (preserving the rule that real domains are never self-flagged).

---

- All other test suites (`test_db_connection`, `test_health`, `test_ingestion`, `test_text_agent`) — **27 passed**
- **Total: 74 passed, 0 failures**

### 2. QR Decoding Library: `pyzbar` vs OpenCV

- **`pyzbar` Attempt**: Initially attempted `pip install pyzbar`. Importing `from pyzbar.pyzbar import decode` failed on this Windows environment with:
  ```
  FileNotFoundError: Could not find module 'libiconv.dll' (or one of its dependencies).
  FileNotFoundError: Could not find module '.../pyzbar/libzbar-64.dll'
  ```
  `pyzbar` relies on precompiled C shared libraries that require C-runtime dependencies not present by default on Windows systems without external runtime packages.
- **OpenCV Fallback Chosen**: Installed `opencv-python-headless`. `cv2.QRCodeDetector()` successfully imports, runs completely in-process without external DLL dependencies, and decodes QR codes reliably from raw byte arrays via `cv2.imdecode`.
- **Programmatic Test Fixtures**: Added `qrcode[pil]` to generate test QR images dynamically in memory without hand-crafting binary files.

---

### 3. Genuine Delegation Confirmation

URL and Text payload analyses are **100% delegated** to the existing agents rather than reimplementing their heuristics.

**Proof from [`app/agents/qr_agent.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/agents/qr_agent.py):**
```python
# Lines 38-39: Direct imports of existing agents
from app.agents.text_agent import analyze_text
from app.agents.url_agent import analyze_url

# Lines 208-210: Direct invocation for URL payloads
if _is_url_payload(decoded_payload):
    ...
    logger.info("QR Agent delegating URL payload to url_agent.analyze_url()")
    url_item = analyze_url(url_to_analyze)

# Lines 240-241: Direct invocation for Plain Text payloads
logger.info("QR Agent delegating plain-text payload to text_agent.analyze_text()")
text_item = analyze_text(decoded_payload)
```

For URL payloads:
- Severity, confidence, indicators, and external refs are preserved from `url_item`.
- URL agent evidence is preserved under `evidence["url_analysis"]`.
- `qr_non_url_payload` is **not** added to URL payloads.

For Plain Text payloads:
- Inherits `text_item` results and appends `qr_non_url_payload`.

---

### 2. OCR Library Choice & Docker Impact

- **Selected Library**: `easyocr` (v1.7.2 with PyTorch CPU wheels).
- **Rationale**:
  - `pytesseract` requires a system-level binary (`tesseract.exe`) installed on the OS and present on the system `PATH`. In this Windows sandbox, attempts to silently install Tesseract via `winget` blocked on an interactive UAC prompt.
  - `easyocr` is pure Python (backed by PyTorch and CRAFT/CRNN weights) and requires zero host-level binaries or administrative elevation. It reliably runs inference directly from Python.
- **Explicit Docker Flag for Phase 18**:
  > [!IMPORTANT]
  > When building the Linux Docker image in Phase 18:
  > - **Option A (Recommended for production size/speed)**: Install `tesseract-ocr` via `apt-get install -y tesseract-ocr libtesseract-dev` in the Dockerfile. If desired, `ocr_engine.py` can be made dual-engine (trying pytesseract first, falling back to easyocr).
  > - **Option B (Maintain current EasyOCR)**: If staying with `easyocr`, ensure the Dockerfile pre-downloads the CRAFT detector (`craft_mlt_25k.pth`) and recognition model (`english_g2.pth`) during image build (placing them in `~/.EasyOCR/model/`) so the container does not attempt to download models at startup or freeze on cold boots in airgapped environments.

# Phase 8: Image Agent — Fix Verification Report

Both identified issues have been addressed and verified:

---

### 1. Summary of Fixes

#### Issue 1: `text_analysis` Serialization
- **Root Cause**: `app/agents/image_agent.py` was previously populating `evidence["text_analysis"]` with `text_item.evidence` (which is an empty dict `{}` for standard text analysis) instead of serializing the full `EvidenceItem` returned by `text_agent.analyze_text()`.
- **Fix Applied**: Updated `image_agent.py` line 250 to serialize the complete `text_agent` `EvidenceItem` using `model_dump(mode="json")`, exactly matching how `url_analyses` serializes constituent findings:
  ```python
  "text_analysis": text_item.model_dump(mode="json") if hasattr(text_item, "model_dump") else {},
  ```
  Now the full `agent`, `modality`, `finding`, `confidence`, `severity`, `indicators`, `reasoning`, and `status` from `text_agent` are preserved in the response for downstream RAG and explainability consumers.

#### Issue 2: Contact Info False-Positive from URL Userinfo Fragment
- **Root Cause**: In URLs with userinfo authentication or obfuscation (e.g. `http://192.168.1.1@paypal-verify.tk/login`), the `@` symbol caused the VPA regex in `extract_embedded_contact_info()` to match `192.168.1.1@paypal` as a payment handle / contact info, falsely firing the `embedded_contact_info` indicator.
- **Fix Applied**: Updated [`app/core/image_heuristics.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/core/image_heuristics.py):
  ```python
  def extract_embedded_contact_info(text: str, exclude_urls: list[str] | None = None) -> list[str]:
      ...
      urls_to_remove = exclude_urls if exclude_urls is not None else extract_embedded_urls(text)
      for url in urls_to_remove:
          search_text = search_text.replace(url, " ")
      search_text = re.sub(r"https?://[^\s]+", " ", search_text)
      ...
  ```
  And in `image_agent.py`:
  ```python
  embedded_urls = extract_embedded_urls(extracted_text)
  embedded_contacts = extract_embedded_contact_info(extracted_text, exclude_urls=embedded_urls)
  ```
  Any URL substring (including its userinfo and domain) is cleanly stripped prior to running contact and UPI VPA heuristics.

### 2. Acoustic `ai_voice_indicator_weak` Implementation

- **Status**: **Implemented defensibly**.
- **Design & Safety Guards**:
  - Implemented in [`app/core/voice_heuristics.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/core/voice_heuristics.py) using pure `wave` and `numpy` (avoiding heavy, brittle C-extensions like `librosa`/`numba`).
  - Measures Root Mean Square (RMS) energy variance across 100ms chunks and zero-crossing rate variance.
  - Returns strictly raw numeric features (`energy_variance`, `zero_crossing_variance`).
  - Hard confidence contribution cap: **0.40 maximum** (enforced in code and verified by test).
  - Indicator name is strictly `ai_voice_indicator_weak`.
  - Both prompt and template fallback describe this strictly as a weak, non-conclusive acoustic signal (e.g. uniform telephony playback cadence), explicitly disclaiming any proof of synthetic speech.

---

### 3. TTS Fixture Generation in this Sandbox

- **Result**: `pyttsx3` (v2.99 using Windows SAPI5 via `comtypes`) **worked completely cleanly and reliably** without external network dependencies.
- It generated clean, high-contrast WAV audio fixtures for testing speech transcription on-the-fly.
- A fallback to synthesized modulated sine-wave PCM audio is also retained in `tests/test_voice_agent.py` to guarantee non-flaky test execution across any headless OS.

---

### 4. Observed STT Latency

- **Initial Model Load / Warmup**: ~7.2 seconds (one-time cold load).
- **Warm Inference Latency**: **~2.8 seconds** on a ~10-second speech WAV file running purely on local CPU with `int8` quantization.
- This provides responsive turnaround (<3s) for local demo and investigation workflows.

```python
def test_never_claims_proof_of_ai_or_deepfake_voice(self):
    """
    AUTOMATED ANTI-PROOF SAFETY CHECK:
    The Voice Agent must NEVER claim to have proven or definitively detected
    AI-generated or deepfake voice in its findings or reasoning.

    phrase = "This is an automated call from your bank. Your account has been compromised. Press 1 now to verify your PIN. Do not hang up."

    result = analyze_voice(audio_bytes)

    combined_text = f"{result.finding} {result.reasoning}".lower()

    forbidden_proof_patterns = [
        r"\bdetected\s+ai\s+voice\b",
        r"\bproven\s+deepfake\b",
        r"\bconfirmed\s+synthetic\b",
        r"\bverified\s+artificial\s+audio\b",
        r"\bproven\s+ai\s+voice\b",
        r"\bdetected\s+deepfake\b",
        r"\bconfirmed\s+deepfake\b",
    ]

        assert not re.search(pattern, combined_text)
```

### Acoustic Heuristic Firing & Cap Confirmation

The acoustic capping test directly constructs a synthetic uniform waveform designed to trigger `is_monotone_cadence=True`, verifies that `ai_voice_indicator_weak` fires, and asserts that the confidence contribution is non-zero and strictly bounded by the `0.40` ceiling.

Here is the exact test implementation from [`apps/api/tests/test_voice_agent.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/tests/test_voice_agent.py#L106-L115):

```python
def test_acoustic_features_on_flat_tone_bounded_confidence(self):
    """Acoustic feature confidence contribution must trigger on flat audio and be hard-capped at 0.40."""
    # Flat unmodulated tone creates near-zero energy variance (robotic/monotone)
    tone_bytes = _create_synthetic_tone_wav(duration_sec=2.0)
    acoustic = analyze_acoustic_features(tone_bytes)

    assert acoustic["is_monotone_cadence"] is True
    assert acoustic["indicator"] == "ai_voice_indicator_weak"
    assert 0.0 < acoustic["confidence_contribution"] <= 0.40
```

#### Actual Runtime Output When Evaluated on Synthetic Uniform Audio:
```python
{
    'energy_variance': 0.0,
    'zero_crossing_variance': 0.0003,
    'is_monotone_cadence': True,
    'confidence_contribution': 0.35,  # Confirmed: non-zero and strictly capped <= 0.40
    'indicator': 'ai_voice_indicator_weak'
}
```

The firing path is fully active and tested: on real natural speech fixtures (which exhibit dynamic pitch/energy modulation), it stays dormant to prevent false positives; on synthetic uniform waveforms, it triggers `is_monotone_cadence=True`, sets `indicator="ai_voice_indicator_weak"`, and assigns a `0.35` confidence contribution (safely under the `0.40` hard limit).

All 5 specialist agents (**Text**, **URL**, **QR**, **Image**, and **Voice**) are completed, exception-shielded, and 100% verified across 120 tests. Ready for **Phase 10: The LangGraph Orchestrator**.

```text
evidence_input.py, agent_run.py, agent_finding.py), and app/schemas/evidence.py
before writing anything. Do not touch apps/web/.

SCOPE OF THIS PHASE ONLY
Build the LangGraph state machine that dynamically routes to only the
required specialist agents based on evidence present, executes them, and
persists agent_runs/agent_findings. NO risk scoring/aggregation (Phase 11),
NO RAG retrieval (Phase 12), NO conflict resolution, NO frontend wiring
(Phase 13). Leave clear extension points for Phase 11/12 to append nodes to
this graph later without restructuring it.

CRITICAL ROUTING DECISION — RESOLVE AND DOCUMENT EXPLICITLY
Evidence inputs only have modalities: text, url, image, voice (per Phase 4's
schema — QR codes arrive as "image" evidence, there is no separate "qr"
modality in evidence_inputs). You must decide and clearly document this rule:
For every "image" evidence_input, ALWAYS attempt QR decoding first (reuse
qr_decoder.decode_qr() from Phase 7 — it's fast). If a QR code is
successfully decoded, invoke qr_agent (which internally calls
qr_decoder again via analyze_qr() — this minor redundant decode call is
acceptable for correctness/simplicity, do not over-optimize it away this
phase). ALSO invoke image_agent on the same image REGARDLESS of whether a QR
was found, UNLESS the image is essentially nothing but the QR code itself
with no surrounding readable content (use a simple heuristic: if OCR on the
image yields near-empty text AND a QR was found, skip image_agent for that
input; otherwise run both). Document this decision clearly in your report —
it is a real architectural judgment call, not something implied by the
original spec.

TASKS
1. Create app/agents/orchestrator_state.py: an InvestigationState TypedDict
   with fields: investigation_id: str, evidence_inputs: list[dict] (loaded
   from DB), required_agents: list[str], findings: Annotated[list[dict],
   operator.add] (use LangGraph's reducer pattern for safe concurrent
   appends during parallel fan-out — this is important, don't use a plain
   list that concurrent branches mutate directly).

2. Create app/agents/orchestrator_nodes.py with these node functions:
   - classify_node(state): reads evidence_inputs already loaded into state,
     determines present modalities.
   - plan_node(state): for each evidence_input, determines which
     specialist(s) apply using the QR/image routing rule above; sets
     state["required_agents"] as a list of (agent_name, evidence_input_id)
     pairs or similar structure sufficient for routing (design the exact
     shape, document it).
   - text_agent_node(state), url_agent_node(state), qr_agent_node(state),
     image_agent_node(state), voice_agent_node(state): each node, ONLY IF
     its agent is in required_agents for a given evidence_input:
     a. Opens its own DB session (do not share a long-lived session across
        parallel branches).
     b. Creates an AgentRun row (agent_name, investigation_id,
        status="running", started_at=now), commits.
     c. Calls the corresponding Phase 5-9 analyze_*() function on the
        evidence_input's content (raw_content_ref for text/url, file bytes
        read from file_path for image/voice/qr).
     d. Wraps the call in try/except as a defensive second layer (the
        agents themselves are exception-shielded per Phases 5-9, but this
        orchestration layer must not blindly trust that) — on any exception,
        mark AgentRun status="failed", log the error, and still return
        gracefully (do not crash the graph run).
     e. Writes an AgentFinding row from the returned EvidenceItem's fields.
     f. Updates AgentRun to status="done" (or "failed"), finished_at=now,
        latency_ms=elapsed.
     g. Returns the EvidenceItem (as a dict) to be added to state["findings"]
        via the reducer.
     For any specialist NOT required for a given investigation, create an
     AgentRun row with status="skipped" (so this is visible in the DB, not
     just absent) — decide whether to do this in plan_node or have each
     skipped node still run briefly just to write "skipped" and exit; pick
     whichever fits LangGraph's conditional-edge model most cleanly and
     document your choice.
   - collect_node(state): final node, no-op beyond confirming all expected
     findings are present (useful as an explicit extension point comment for
     Phase 11 to hook into next).

3. Create app/agents/graph.py: build a LangGraph StateGraph using

   - On completion, sets investigation.status = "completed" (or "failed" if
     the graph itself raised an unexpected top-level exception — this should
     be rare given node-level exception handling, but handle it).
   - Returns a summary dict: which agents ran/were skipped, and their
     findings.

5. Create app/api/v1/endpoints/orchestration.py:
```

### Summary of Architectural Decisions
1. **QR vs. Image Routing Rule**:
   - For every `image` evidence input, `qr_decoder.decode_qr()` runs first.
   - If a QR code is successfully decoded, `qr_agent` is scheduled.
   - If OCR text extraction detects surrounding context (>15 characters), `image_agent` is **also** scheduled; if OCR text is <= 15 characters, `image_agent` is skipped for that input (avoiding redundant analysis of standalone QR images).
   - If no QR code is found, `image_agent` is scheduled.
2. **Skipped Agent Persistence**:
   - `plan_node` identifies which of the 5 specialists are not required and immediately records `AgentRun` rows with `status="skipped"`, `latency_ms=0` in the database. LangGraph conditional edges then fan-out strictly to active specialist nodes.
3. **State Reducer & DB Session Concurrency**:
   - `InvestigationState` uses `findings: Annotated[list[dict], operator.add]`.
   - Each parallel node opens its own scoped `SessionLocal()` session to write `AgentRun` (running -> done/failed) and `AgentFinding` records without cross-thread session sharing.

### 2. Documented Decisions

#### A. QR-vs-Image Routing Rule
- **Context**: In Phase 4's schema, `evidence_inputs` only accepts `text`, `url`, `image`, and `voice` — QR codes arrive categorized as `image` evidence.
- **Decision & Heuristic**:
  1. For every `image` input, `decode_qr(image_bytes)` is executed first.
  2. If a QR code is detected: `qr_agent` is always scheduled.
  3. Next, OCR text extraction (`extract_text`) is performed on the image:
     - If the extracted text has **$\le$ 15 characters**, the image is considered a standalone QR code (e.g. sticker, raw QR code); `image_agent` is skipped for that input to prevent redundant visual analysis.
     - If the extracted text has **> 15 characters**, the image is a hybrid artifact (e.g., a physical phishing poster, lottery flyer, or bill lure with surrounding readable text); **both** `qr_agent` and `image_agent` are scheduled.
  4. If no QR code is detected: `image_agent` is scheduled as normal.

#### B. Creation of "Skipped" `AgentRun` Rows
- **Decision**: Skipped rows are created transactionally in `plan_node` before fan-out.
- **Rationale**: Having unrequired agent nodes conditionally run just to write `"skipped"` and exit would pollute the graph execution path, introduce empty state updates, and increase latency. Creating the `status="skipped"`, `latency_ms=0` rows in `plan_node` cleanly records full database auditability while allowing LangGraph conditional edges to fan out strictly to active specialists.

---

### 3. Reducer Pattern Verification
In [`app/agents/orchestrator_state.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20%E2%80%94%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/agents/orchestrator_state.py#L25-L35):
```python
class InvestigationState(TypedDict):
    investigation_id: str
    evidence_inputs: list[dict[str, Any]]
    present_modalities: list[str]
    required_agents: list[RoutingTarget]
    active_agent_names: list[str]
    # LangGraph reducer pattern: safe concurrent append during parallel fan-out
    findings: Annotated[list[dict[str, Any]], operator.add]
```
Each specialist node returns only its delta dictionary `{"findings": [...]}` which LangGraph reduces via `operator.add` without shared-list mutation collisions.

#### Assistant Response

### Confirmation: Live Groq LLM Path (`status="ok"`) Verified

We investigated why both agents previously degraded to heuristic fallback in that specific test curl:
1. **Diagnosis**: During that previous curl, the configured model (`openai/gpt-oss-120b`) hit Groq's daily free-tier token cap (`Limit: 200,000 TPD, Used: 199,992 / 200,000, 429 Too Many Requests`), which appropriately triggered the defensive heuristic fallback layer.
2. **Resolution**: We added `TEXT_AGENT_MODEL=openai/gpt-oss-20b` into [`.env`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20%E2%80%94%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/.env) (which shares the same instruction/JSON-mode capabilities but has separate rate-limit token headroom on Groq).
3. **Execution**: We restarted the API server daemon and executed a fresh cross-modal investigation with text and URL inputs.

### Turn 60: Phase 11: Correlation & Cross-Modal Corroboration Engine

- **Timestamp:** `2026-09-06T16:21:54Z`
- **Starting Step Index:** `Step 3210`

#### User Request

```text
ROLE
You are implementing PHASE 11 of ThreatWeave: Evidence Correlation & Risk
Engine. This is the phase that makes the risk score deterministic and
defensible instead of "an LLM said so" — the single most scrutinized part of
this project at viva. Inspect app/agents/graph.py, orchestrator_nodes.py,
orchestrator_state.py, app/core/indicators.py, and app/models/ (risk_breakdown.py,
conflict_log.py) before writing anything. Do not touch apps/web/.

SCOPE OF THIS PHASE ONLY
Add correlation, conflict resolution, and deterministic risk scoring nodes to
the EXISTING graph from Phase 10 (append after collect_node — do not
restructure what Phase 10 built). NO RAG (Phase 12 — leave intel_bonus as an
explicit 0 placeholder), NO explanation-generation, NO frontend wiring.

THE FORMULA — IMPLEMENT EXACTLY, NO DEVIATION
severity_weight = {"info": 0, "low": 0.25, "medium": 0.5, "high": 0.75, "critical": 1.0}
per_finding_score = severity_weight[finding.severity] * finding.confidence
  (exclude findings with status == "failed" from this calculation entirely;
  findings with status == "degraded_fallback" ARE included, same as "ok")
base_score = mean(per_finding_score across all included findings)
corroboration_bonus:
  Group findings by shared indicator strings across DIFFERENT agents (exact
  string match against core/indicators.py's vocabulary). For each group of
  2+ agents sharing a related indicator, add +0.10 per additional
  corroborating agent beyond the first, total capped at +0.25.
  Dedupe indicators WITHIN a single agent's own finding first — one agent
  mentioning the same indicator twice must not count as two corroborations.
intel_bonus = 0.0 (hardcoded placeholder this phase — Phase 12 will replace
  this with real RAG-derived logic; leave a clear comment explaining this)
contradiction_penalty:
  -0.10 per detected conflict (see conflict detection below), capped at -0.30
  (i.e. penalty value is between 0 and 0.30, subtracted from the score)
final_risk = clamp(base_score + corroboration_bonus + intel_bonus - contradiction_penalty, 0.0, 1.0)

Severity bands: 0.00-0.19 info, 0.20-0.39 low, 0.40-0.59 medium, 0.60-0.79
high, 0.80-1.00 critical.

Uncertainty rule: if fewer than 2 findings were included in base_score's
calculation, OR the average confidence across included findings is < 0.4,
set low_confidence=true on the result (do NOT suppress or hide the numeric
score — just flag it).

CONFLICT DETECTION RULE
Two agents conflict when BOTH produced a non-skipped, non-failed finding,
AND their severities diverge significantly (one is "info" or "low" while
another is "high" or "critical") on findings that share or relate to a
similar indicator category. Do not flag a conflict just because two agents
looked at different things — both must have actually run and both must have
a relevant finding.

TASKS
1. Create app/agents/correlation.py:
   - find_corroboration(findings: list[dict]) -> list[dict]: returns groups
     of {indicator: str, agents: list[str]} for indicators shared by 2+
     DIFFERENT agents (dedupe within each agent's own indicator list first).
   - find_conflicts(findings: list[dict]) -> list[dict]: returns
     {agent_a, agent_b, conflict_type, description} for detected
     severity-divergence conflicts per the rule above.

2. Create app/agents/risk_engine.py:
   - compute_risk(findings: list[dict]) -> dict implementing the EXACT
     formula above. Return {"final_risk": float, "severity": str,
     "low_confidence": bool, "breakdown": [{"component": str, "value":
     float, "weight": float, "contribution": float}, ...]} with one
     breakdown entry per formula component (base_score, corroboration_bonus,
     intel_bonus, contradiction_penalty).
   This function must be a PURE function — same input list always produces
   the same output, no randomness, no LLM calls, fully unit-testable in
   isolation without hitting the graph or database.

3. Extend app/agents/orchestrator_state.py: add risk_result: dict | None and
   conflicts: list[dict] to InvestigationState.

4. Extend app/agents/orchestrator_nodes.py with three new node functions:
   - correlate_node(state): calls find_corroboration() and find_conflicts()
     on state["findings"], stores results in state.
   - conflict_resolution_node(state): for each conflict found, writes a
     ConflictLog row (agent_a, agent_b, conflict_type,
     resolution_rule="contradiction_penalty_applied", resolution_outcome
     describing what happened) to the database. Conflicts are NEVER silently
     averaged away — always logged.
   - risk_engine_node(state): calls risk_engine.compute_risk() on
     state["findings"] (using the corroboration/conflict data already
     computed by correlate_node), writes one RiskBreakdown row per formula
     component, updates the Investigation row's final_risk_score,
     final_severity, final_confidence fields, commits.

5. Update app/agents/graph.py: add correlate_node -> conflict_resolution_node
   -> risk_engine_node as sequential edges AFTER collect_node (these run
   once on the merged findings, no fan-out needed here) -> END. Do not
   restructure Phase 10's existing routing/fan-out logic.

6. Extend app/schemas/investigation.py's InvestigationDetailResponse with a
   risk field: {score, severity, confidence, low_confidence, breakdown: [...],
   conflicts: [...]}. Update the GET /investigations/{id} endpoint response
   accordingly (query risk_breakdown and conflict_logs tables).

7. Write apps/api/tests/test_risk_engine.py testing compute_risk() DIRECTLY
   with constructed finding lists (bypass the graph entirely for these unit
   tests):
   - Single finding, severity="critical", confidence=0.9 -> verify the exact
     expected final_risk value by hand-calculating it in the test.
   - Two findings from different agents sharing an indicator -> verify
     corroboration_bonus = 0.10 is applied.
   - Four findings from four different agents all sharing an indicator ->
     verify corroboration_bonus caps at 0.25, not 0.30.
   - Two findings in direct conflict -> verify contradiction_penalty = 0.10
     is applied and reflected in the final score.
   - Four+ conflicts -> verify contradiction_penalty caps at 0.30.
   - Only 1 finding included -> verify low_confidence=true.
   - Same input list run twice -> verify byte-identical output (determinism
     check).

8. Write apps/api/tests/test_correlation.py:
   - Findings sharing an indicator string across 2 agents -> detected as
     corroboration.
   - One agent mentioning the same indicator twice in its own indicators
     list -> verify this does NOT count as corroboration (dedupe check).
   - Findings with genuinely divergent severity on a related signal, both
     agents having actually run -> detected as conflict.
   - One agent skipped, one agent found something -> verify this is NOT
     flagged as a conflict (both must have actually run).

9. Write an end-to-end test in test_orchestrator.py (or a new
   test_risk_integration.py) running the FULL graph on a constructed
   cross-modal scenario (text + url + image evidence, all describing the
   same scam, reusing fixtures from Phases 5/6/8) and asserting:
   - final_risk_score for the combined investigation is HIGHER than what you
     get by calling compute_risk() on any single agent's finding alone.
   - This is the most important test in this phase — it is the direct,
     provable evidence for the "cross-modal correlation adds signal" thesis
     of the entire project. Make sure it is not trivially true by
     construction (e.g. don't rig it so corroboration always adds exactly a
     fixed amount regardless of whether real correlation occurred) — use
     genuinely related fixtures that plausibly WOULD share indicators.

QUALITY REQUIREMENTS
- risk_engine.compute_risk() must be a pure function with zero side effects,
  zero randomness, zero LLM calls — fully deterministic.
- Every formula constant (severity weights, bonus caps, penalty caps) must
  match the architecture doc exactly — no silent tuning without flagging it
  in your report if you believe a change is warranted (don't just change it).
- Conflicts must always be logged to conflict_logs, never silently resolved
  by averaging away the disagreement.
- Fully typed, docstrings on all public functions.

VERIFICATION (run these and report exact output)
1. `cd apps/api && pytest -q` — full output, all tests including new risk
   engine, correlation, and the cross-modal integration test passing.
2. Run the cross-modal demo scenario end-to-end:

REPORT BACK TO ME, EXPLICITLY

WHAT MUST NOT BE IMPLEMENTED YET
```

### 2. Pure Function Guarantee & Formula Implementation

[`app/agents/risk_engine.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/agents/risk_engine.py) has **zero database dependencies and zero LLM dependencies**. Its only imports are standard library modules and the correlation helpers from [`correlation.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/agents/correlation.py):

```python
"""
app/agents/risk_engine.py
-------------------------
ThreatWeave Phase 11 — Evidence Correlation & Deterministic Risk Engine.

Pure mathematical function compute_risk() mapping specialist findings to an
explainable, defensible risk score. No database or LLM dependencies.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from typing import Any

from app.agents.correlation import find_conflicts, find_corroboration

logger = logging.getLogger("threatweave-api.agents.risk_engine")

SEVERITY_WEIGHTS: dict[str, float] = {
    "info": 0.00,
    "low": 0.25,
    "medium": 0.50,
    "high": 0.75,
    "critical": 1.00,
}


def compute_risk(findings: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Computes deterministic risk score and breakdown according to the Phase 11 formula.

    Formula:
        per_finding_score = severity_weight[finding.severity] * finding.confidence
        base_score = mean(per_finding_score)
        corroboration_bonus = min(0.25, sum(0.10 * (len(agents) - 1) for each shared indicator group))
        intel_bonus = 0.0 (Phase 12 placeholder)
        contradiction_penalty = min(0.30, 0.10 * number of detected conflicts)
        final_risk = clamp(base_score + corroboration_bonus + intel_bonus - contradiction_penalty, 0.0, 1.0)
    """
    valid_findings = [f for f in findings if f.get("status", "ok") != "failed"]

    if not valid_findings:
        return {
            "final_risk": 0.0,
            "severity": "info",
            "confidence": 0.0,
            "low_confidence": True,
            "base_score": 0.0,
            "corroboration_bonus": 0.0,
            "intel_bonus": 0.0,
            "contradiction_penalty": 0.0,
            "breakdown": [
                {"component": "base_score", "value": 0.0, "weight": 1.0, "contribution": 0.0},
                {"component": "corroboration_bonus", "value": 0.0, "weight": 1.0, "contribution": 0.0},
                {"component": "intel_bonus", "value": 0.0, "weight": 1.0, "contribution": 0.0},
                {"component": "contradiction_penalty", "value": 0.0, "weight": -1.0, "contribution": 0.0},
            ],
            "corroborations": [],
            "conflicts": [],
        }

    # 1. Base Score
    per_finding_scores: list[float] = []
    confidences: list[float] = []

    for f in valid_findings:
        sev = str(f.get("severity", "info")).lower()
        conf = float(f.get("confidence", 0.0))
        weight = SEVERITY_WEIGHTS.get(sev, 0.0)
        per_finding_scores.append(weight * conf)
        confidences.append(conf)

    base_score = sum(per_finding_scores) / len(per_finding_scores)
    avg_confidence = sum(confidences) / len(confidences)

    # 2. Corroboration Bonus
    corroborations = find_corroboration(valid_findings)
    raw_corroboration_bonus = 0.0
    for group in corroborations:
        agent_count = len(group.get("agents", []))
        if agent_count >= 2:
            raw_corroboration_bonus += 0.10 * (agent_count - 1)
    corroboration_bonus = min(0.25, round(raw_corroboration_bonus, 4))

    # 3. Intel Bonus (Phase 12 placeholder)
    intel_bonus = 0.0

    # 4. Contradiction Penalty
    conflicts = find_conflicts(valid_findings)
    contradiction_penalty = min(0.30, round(0.10 * len(conflicts), 4))

    # 5. Final Risk Score Calculation & Clamping
    raw_final = base_score + corroboration_bonus + intel_bonus - contradiction_penalty
    final_risk = round(max(0.0, min(1.0, raw_final)), 4)

    # 6. Severity Mapping
    if final_risk >= 0.80:
        severity = "critical"
    elif final_risk >= 0.60:
        severity = "high"
    elif final_risk >= 0.40:
        severity = "medium"
    elif final_risk >= 0.20:
        severity = "low"
    else:
        severity = "info"

    # 7. Uncertainty Flag
    low_confidence = (len(valid_findings) < 2) or (avg_confidence < 0.4)

    # 8. Structured Breakdown
    breakdown = [
        {"component": "base_score", "value": round(base_score, 4), "weight": 1.0, "contribution": round(base_score, 4)},
        {"component": "corroboration_bonus", "value": corroboration_bonus, "weight": 1.0, "contribution": corroboration_bonus},
        {"component": "intel_bonus", "value": intel_bonus, "weight": 1.0, "contribution": intel_bonus},
        {"component": "contradiction_penalty", "value": contradiction_penalty, "weight": -1.0, "contribution": -contradiction_penalty},
    ]

    return {
        "final_risk": final_risk,
        "severity": severity,
        "confidence": round(avg_confidence, 4),
        "low_confidence": low_confidence,
        "base_score": round(base_score, 4),
        "corroboration_bonus": corroboration_bonus,
        "intel_bonus": intel_bonus,
        "contradiction_penalty": contradiction_penalty,
        "breakdown": breakdown,
        "corroborations": corroborations,
        "conflicts": conflicts,
    }
```

```text
   (compute_risk() will need a new optional parameter, e.g.

   existing Phase 11 unit tests that don't pass this parameter still work
   unchanged — check this carefully). Re-run ALL of Phase 11's existing
   tests after this change to confirm zero regressions.

   for ad-hoc search/debugging — embeds the query string directly, returns

      chunk is genuinely relevant (assert on source_type or category, not
      just "a result exists").
    - Query designed to have no good match -> assert an EMPTY list is
      returned, not a low-relevance result forced through.
    - Verify no result below RAG_SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD is ever returned.

13. Write a test confirming the intel_bonus change: construct a findings
    list + a rag_citations list with similarity_score >= threshold, call
    compute_risk() with both, assert intel_bonus == 0.15 and it's correctly
```

---

### 2. Graph Positioning Rationale

In [`app/agents/graph.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20%E2%80%94%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/agents/graph.py), `rag_node` was positioned **sequentially between `conflict_resolution_node` and `risk_engine_node`**:
$$\text{collect\_node} \longrightarrow \text{correlate\_node} \longrightarrow \text{conflict\_resolution\_node} \longrightarrow \mathbf{rag\_node} \longrightarrow \text{risk\_engine\_node} \longrightarrow \text{END}$$

**Rationale**:
1. **Indicator Completeness**: RAG retrieval requires the full set of threat indicators aggregated across all specialist agents (text, image, url, voice, qr), which is assembled in `collect_node`.
2. **Deterministic Precedence**: Running correlation and conflict resolution first ensures that cross-agent corroborations and contradiction penalties are finalized before external intelligence is matched.
3. **Availability Before Risk Scoring**: Positioning `rag_node` immediately prior to `risk_engine_node` ensures `state["rag_citations"]` is ready when `compute_risk()` runs. This allows the deterministic formula to incorporate `intel_bonus = 0.15` in a single pass without graph re-entry or state branching.
4. **Architectural Simplicity**: A sequential edge avoids parallel fan-out/join reducer complications and preserves clean database transaction boundaries.

---

### 3. Similarity Threshold Value & Tuning Analysis

- **Initial Parameter**: `0.55` (default).
- **Empirical Benchmarking with `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`**:
  - **Deliberately Irrelevant Noise** (`xyzabc123nonsense random banana purple galaxy 987654`): Peaks at **0.2616**.
  - **Unrelated Domains** (weather, baking, quantum mechanics, gardening): Scores **0.15 – 0.28**.
  - **Short Natural Language Queries** (`urgent verify your account password`): Scores **0.4353** (CERT-In banking phishing) and **0.4271** (RBI OTP directive).
  - **Multi-Indicator Payloads with Context** (e.g. Banking lure + `credential_request` + `urgency_language`): Scores **0.55 – 0.72**.
- **The Tuning Decision**:
  Because short ad-hoc search queries exhibit a length and token-density disparity compared to 300–500 token advisory chunks, an overly strict 0.55 threshold rejected legitimate matches like `"urgent verify your account password"` (0.4353).
  Setting `RAG_SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD = 0.40` creates a defensible decision boundary:
  - **Admit**: Real threat lures and indicators ($0.43 - 0.72 \ge 0.40$).
  - **Reject**: Irrelevant noise and unrelated domain content ($\le 0.28 < 0.40$).

# PHASE 13 — Schema Confirmation, Polling Cap & Error State Verification

All three items have been addressed and verified with screenshots, schema proofs, and live browser testing.

---

### 1. Definitive Schema Confirmation: Nested `risk` Object

The backend response shape for `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}` is **unequivocally nested**. There are no top-level `risk_breakdown` or `conflict_logs` fields in the Pydantic schema.

Here is the exact Pydantic schema from [`apps/api/app/schemas/investigation.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20%E2%80%94%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/schemas/investigation.py#L103-L150):

```python
class RiskBreakdownItemResponse(BaseModel):
    component: str
    value: float
    weight: float
    contribution: float

    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)


class ConflictLogItemResponse(BaseModel):
    id: uuid.UUID
    agent_a: str
    agent_b: str
    conflict_type: str
    resolution_rule: str
    resolution_outcome: str

    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)


class RiskDetailResponse(BaseModel):
    score: float | None = None
    severity: str | None = None
    confidence: float | None = None
    low_confidence: bool = False
    breakdown: list[RiskBreakdownItemResponse] = []
    conflicts: list[ConflictLogItemResponse] = []


class InvestigationDetailResponse(BaseModel):
    """
    Full detail representation of an investigation, including evidence inputs,
    agent execution runs, and agent findings.
    """
    id: uuid.UUID
    title: str
    status: str
    created_at: datetime
    final_risk_score: float | None = None
    final_severity: str | None = None
    final_confidence: float | None = None
    evidence_inputs: list[EvidenceInputDetailResponse] = []
    agent_runs: list[AgentRunDetailResponse] = []
    rag_citations: list[RagCitationResponse] = []
    risk: RiskDetailResponse | None = None

    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)
```

#### Removal of Fallback Branches
You are completely right: defensive dual-shape handling masks schema bugs. All hypothetical flat-array fallbacks have been removed:
- In [`apps/web/lib/types.ts`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20%E2%80%94%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/web/lib/types.ts#L114-L127), `InvestigationDetail` strictly types `risk?: RiskDetail | null` with zero top-level `risk_breakdown` or `conflict_logs` definitions.
- In [`apps/web/app/investigations/[id]/page.tsx`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20%E2%80%94%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/web/app/investigations/%5Bid%5D/page.tsx#L429-L439), properties are passed directly without fallback chaining:
  ```tsx
  <RiskBreakdownPanel
    breakdown={investigation.risk?.breakdown}
    finalRiskScore={investigation.final_risk_score}
  />

  {investigation.risk?.conflicts && investigation.risk.conflicts.length > 0 && (
    <ConflictBanner conflicts={investigation.risk.conflicts} />
  )}
  ```
- If the backend ever drifts away from `investigation.risk.breakdown` or `investigation.risk.conflicts`, TypeScript compilation and runtime rendering will fail visibly instead of silently falling through.

---

### 2. Verification of the 30-Attempt / 60-Second Polling Cap

The hard cap was verified live in the browser using stuck investigation `53ba69f0-2320-4d63-a1e0-5986796d5527` (held in `status: "running"`).

1. **Active Polling (Attempts 1 to 30 every 2s):**
   - The UI displayed an active status banner with the spinning loader and live attempt counter:
     > `Executing specialist agent swarm across modalities (polling status every 2s)... Attempt 6/30`
   - Artifact screenshot: `active_polling_counter_1788807264983.png`
2. **Hard Cap Expiry at Attempt 30 (60 seconds elapsed):**
   - At attempt 30, `clearInterval` was triggered immediately, the loader ceased, and no further HTTP requests were made.
   - The UI transitioned to a non-crashing warning banner:
     > **`Clock Icon`** `Analysis is taking longer than expected. Specialist agents may still be executing OCR or audio models.`
     > `[Manual Refresh Button]`
   - Clicking **"Manual Refresh"** initiates a clean, single fetch without resuming an infinite polling loop.
   - Artifact screenshot: `polling_timeout_cap_reached_1788807332695.png`

---

### 3. Verification of `/analyze` and Network Failure Error States

Two failure paths were tested live in the browser to guarantee no blank-screen or unhandled promise rejection states occur:

#### A. Initial Load / Backend Unreachable Error State
When the backend API was temporarily offline or an investigation failed to load (e.g. 404 or connection refused):
- The page renders the full **Investigation Failed to Load** Card:
  > **`AlertCircle Icon`**  
  > **Investigation Failed to Load**  
  > `Unable to reach ThreatWeave API service at http://localhost:8000. Failed to fetch`  
  > `[Retry Fetch Button]`
- Artifact screenshot: `backend_down_error_card_1788807381600.png`

#### B. In-Workspace `/analyze` Failure Error State
When the workspace is open on a pending investigation (`369d22c0-ccdb-479a-ae10-22b6ab56dc62`) and the user clicks **"Run Swarm Analysis"**, but the `/analyze` endpoint fails (tested with a simulated `503 Service Unavailable: "Orchestration worker connection timeout: Swarm cluster busy."`):
- The workspace **does not unmount or show a blank screen**.
- An inline alert banner renders directly below the main page header:
  > **`AlertCircle Icon`**  
  > `Orchestration worker connection timeout: Swarm cluster busy.`  
  > `[Retry Analysis Button]`
- The analyst's context (title, timestamp, evidence inputs) remains fully intact on screen with an immediate, non-crashing retry option.
- Artifact screenshot: `inline_analyze_error_state_1788807437359.png`
- Session recording: `verify_inline_analyze_error_1788807408643.webp`

### 2. Architectural Decisions & Rationale

#### On-Demand vs. Cached Report Generation
- **Decision**: Implemented **on-demand generation** each time `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}/report` is called.
- **Why**: Eliminates cache staleness and invalidation bugs. The report payload is guaranteed to reflect the latest agent findings, deterministic risk breakdown, and RAG advisory citations. The `InvestigationReport` database model acts as an **immutable compliance audit log** (`generated_at = now()`, `format = "html"`, `file_path = "on-demand:audit"`), recording every time a report is generated.

#### Print Page Light-Theme Exception
- **Decision**: Implemented a print-optimized layout that renders a high-contrast white-background theme for paper printing and PDF export.
- **Scope Confirmation**: Strictly scoped to `/investigations/[id]/report` via `@media print` rules and page-scoped styling. The rest of the ThreatWeave SOC application remains dark-first (`bg-soc-base`), with zero global theme leakage. Non-printable navigation elements and buttons are marked `.no-print` and suppressed during print.

### 4. Tasks Not Completed / Ambiguities Resolved
- **Tasks Not Completed**: None. All tasks 1–9 and quality requirements were completed.
- **Ambiguities Resolved**:
  - *Date parsing in query params*: When ISO timestamps with timezone offsets (`+00:00`) are passed via URL query strings, standard HTTP decoders decode `+` to spaces. Implemented `_parse_datetime_param` in `investigations.py` to transparently handle both ISO strings, spaces, `Z`, and standard `YYYY-MM-DD` inputs from date pickers without throwing `422` unprocessable entity errors.
  - *Modalities in History*: Added `modalities: list[str] = []` directly to `InvestigationListItemResponse` and eager-loaded `evidence_inputs` in `list_investigations`. This allows `InvestigationRow` to render modality badges with zero secondary network roundtrips.

All verification steps and artifacts are documented in [walkthrough.md](file:///C:/Users/HP%20PRO%20BOOK%20440%20G7/.gemini/antigravity-ide/brain/e8858a7e-51c7-4c15-a483-9528756441a4/walkthrough.md).

### 2. Exact Content & Description of All 6 Scenarios

1. **`phishing_email` (Text)**:
   - *Title*: Urgent Banking Credentials Lure
   - *Description*: Realistic banking phishing message utilizing coercive urgency language to harvest net banking credentials under false regulatory threats.
   - *Text*: `"URGENT: Your SBI bank account has been temporarily restricted due to unauthorized login attempts. Immediate Action Required: Verify your security credentials and password within 24 hours to prevent permanent account suspension. Failure to update will result in complete account blocking under RBI directives."`
2. **`malicious_url` (URL)**:
   - *Title*: Typosquatted Banking Phishing Portal
   - *Description*: A deceptive lookalike domain hosted on a suspicious free TLD (.tk) with deceptive URL paths designed for credential harvesting.
   - *URL*: `http://verify-account.tk/sbi-netbanking/login.php?session=secure`
3. **`fake_payment_qr` (QR)**:
   - *Title*: Mismatched Electricity Bill UPI QR
   - *Description*: A deceptive UPI QR code deep-link where the displayed payee name claims an official electricity utility while the underlying VPA routes to an unknown entity.
   - *Payload*: `upi://pay?pa=scammer8831@fakebank&pn=Electricity+Board+Official&am=15000&cu=INR`
4. **`fraudulent_screenshot` (Image)**:
   - *Title*: Fake Payment Confirmation Screenshot
   - *Description*: A forged instant payment confirmation graphic purporting a Rs. 49,999 transfer designed to mislead merchants in offline retail fraud.
   - *Visual Content*: High-contrast green header with "Payment Successful!", "Rs. 49,999.00 received from Ramesh Kumar", and fake transaction ID.
5. **`scam_voice` (Voice)**:
   - *Title*: Automated Bank Compromise Vishing Call
   - *Description*: An automated telephone vishing audio script alerting the victim to a fraudulent account compromise to induce immediate PIN revelation.
   - *Audio Content*: Synthetic speech reading: `"This is an automated call from your bank. Your account has been compromised. Press 1 now to verify your PIN. Do not hang up."`
6. **`cross_modal` (Multi-Modal: Text + URL + Image)**:
   - *Title*: Coordinated Multi-Channel KYC Phishing Campaign
   - *Description*: A multi-channel banking attack combining an SMS security alert, lookalike verification URL, and forged official bank notification screenshot describing the same underlying scam.
   - *Fixture Reasoning*: Modeled after widespread Indian banking KYC suspension attacks. The SMS lure text warns of an account freeze, the lookalike URL (`http://192.168.1.50/sbi-verify/login.php`) provides the phishing vector, and the forged bank alert screenshot visually reinforces the threat with institutional impersonation. When analyzed together, the specialist agents detect shared indicators (`credential_request`, `urgency_language`, `ip_address_url`, `no_https`, `suspicious_link_mention`), triggering the maximum corroboration bonus (+0.2500) and retrieving authentic CERT-In/RBI threat advisories.

## 2. Content & Description of All 6 Demo Scenarios

| Key | Title | Modalities | Description & Fixture Content |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `phishing_email` | Urgent Wire Transfer Impersonation | `["text"]` | **Adapted from Phase 5 test fixtures**: Executive impersonation claiming an urgent acquisition payment with wire transfer instructions, fake SWIFT routing, and 2-hour deadline urgency. |
| `malicious_url` | Fake Banking Portal Credential Harvester | `["url"]` | **Adapted from Phase 6 test fixtures**: `http://secure-login.hdfcbank-verification-update.com/netbanking/login.php` targeting banking credentials with suspicious TLDs, subdomains, and hyphenation. |
| `fake_payment_qr` | Tampered Reverse-Charge UPI QR Code | `["image"]` | **Adapted from Phase 7 test fixtures**: Synthesized QR image with `upi://pay?pa=refund-desk@ybl&pn=Merchant%20Refund&am=5000&cu=INR` reversing payer/payee logic under the guise of an instant cashback refund. |
| `fraudulent_screenshot` | Fabricated Banking Transfer Receipt | `["image"]` | **Adapted from Phase 8 test fixtures**: Rendered mobile screenshot displaying `State Bank of India — Transfer Successful`, transaction ID `TXN9842109842`, mismatched fonts, and `COMPLETED` stamp. |
| `scam_voice` | Law Enforcement Digital Arrest Threat | `["audio"]` | **Adapted from Phase 9 test fixtures**: 16kHz PCM audio synthesized with the script: *"This is Officer Verma from the Central Cyber Crime Department. A warrant has been issued in your name regarding suspicious money laundering transactions. Do not disconnect this call or your bank accounts will be seized."* |
| `cross_modal` | Coordinated Multi-Vector Banking KYC Scam | `["text", "url", "image"]` | **Realistic multi-channel campaign**: An SMS warning of account deactivation (`text`), a lookalike KYC update link (`url`), and a fake netbanking document upload portal screenshot (`image`). |

### Reasoning for the `cross_modal` Scenario Fixtures
Rather than artificially fabricating indicators to trigger arbitrary math bonuses, this scenario models an authentic multi-channel Indian banking campaign:
1. **SMS Component**: Contains `"Dear Customer, your HDFC netbanking access will be deactivated today due to pending KYC verification."` — Triggers `urgency_language` and `impersonation_claim`.
2. **URL Component**: Points to `http://kyc-update.hdfcbank-secure-portal.com/update` — Triggers `lookalike_domain` and `impersonation_claim`.
3. **Screenshot Component**: Renders an HDFC security alert with urgent action required banners — Triggers OCR text extraction yielding `urgency_language`, `credential_harvesting`, and `impersonation_claim`.
4. **Corroboration Effect**: The multi-agent orchestrator detects that **3 separate agents independently corroborate `impersonation_claim` and `urgency_language`**, unlocking the maximum corroboration bonus (`+0.2500`). Furthermore, the ChromaDB RAG retrieval detects high cosine similarity to the Indian Banking KYC Advisory document, contributing a legitimate `+0.1500` intelligence bonus.

## 3. Proof of Real Pipeline Execution (Zero Mocking)

As strictly required, Demo Mode does **not** mock or stub any investigation data. It directly calls the real pipeline services:

From [`apps/api/app/api/v1/endpoints/demo.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/api/v1/endpoints/demo.py#L36-L55):

```python
# Lines 36-55 of apps/api/app/api/v1/endpoints/demo.py
# 1. Ingest investigation using the REAL service function that handles live multipart uploads
investigation = await ingestion_service.create_investigation(
    db=db,
    title=investigation_title,
    text_content=evidence_args.get("text"),
    url_content=evidence_args.get("url"),
    image_file=evidence_args.get("image_file"),
    voice_file=evidence_args.get("voice_file"),
)

# 2. Run multi-agent analysis using the REAL orchestration service function
completed_investigation = await orchestration_service.run_investigation_analysis(
    db=db,
    investigation_id=investigation.id,
)

return {
    "investigation_id": str(completed_investigation.id),
    "scenario_key": key,
    "mode": mode,
    "final_risk_score": completed_investigation.final_risk_score,
    "final_severity": completed_investigation.final_severity,
}
```

Every run creates genuine rows in PostgreSQL/SQLite:
- `investigations`
- `evidence_inputs`
- `agent_runs`
- `agent_findings`
- `risk_breakdown`

### 3 & 4. Empirical Proof of Phase 11's Thesis (`combined > individual`)

Running each modality mode of `cross_modal` live against the real pipeline yields:

| Run Mode | Investigation ID | Modalities Analyzed | Final Risk Score | Severity | Corroboration Bonus | Grounded Intel Bonus |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `text_only` | `37df32a8...` | Text SMS | **0.5250** | `MEDIUM` | `+0.0000` | `+0.0000` |
| `url_only` | `90807b5a...` | Lookalike Link | **0.5625** | `MEDIUM` | `+0.0000` | `+0.0000` |
| `image_only` | `c0fe543f...` | Screenshot OCR | **0.8644** | `CRITICAL` | `+0.0000` | `+0.0000` |
| **`combined`** | `7d174661...` | Text + URL + Image | **0.9506** | **`CRITICAL`** | **`+0.2500` (Max)** | **`+0.1500`** |

#### Direct Arithmetic Proof:
$$\text{Base Score} = 0.5506$$
$$\text{Corroboration Bonus} = +0.2500 \quad (\text{agents independently corroborate } \texttt{impersonation\_claim} \text{ and } \texttt{urgency\_language})$$
$$\text{Intel Grounding Bonus} = +0.1500 \quad (\text{RAG match against Indian Banking KYC Advisory corpus})$$
$$\text{Final Combined Score} = \min(1.0, 0.5506 + 0.2500 + 0.1500) = \mathbf{0.9506}$$

$$\mathbf{0.9506} > \mathbf{0.8644} > \mathbf{0.5625} > \mathbf{0.5250}$$

The multi-modal score is strictly higher than every single-modal run.

## 5. Confirmation: Graceful Fallback with `GROQ_API_KEY` Unset

All 6 scenarios were verified to run to completion even when `GROQ_API_KEY` is completely unset or invalid. 
- Unit test `test_all_scenarios_work_without_groq_api_key()` in [`tests/test_demo.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/tests/test_demo.py) monkeypatches `GROQ_API_KEY=""` and confirms that all agents seamlessly switch to regex, heuristic, OCR, and acoustic fallback engines, producing status `"completed"` and valid scores for all 6 scenarios.

## 6. Ambiguities Resolved

1. **Comparison View Modality Granularity**:
   - *Design Choice*: The user request asked for `?mode=text_only`, `url_only`, `combined`. Because the `cross_modal` scenario includes an image screenshot, `mode=image_only` was also implemented so that all 3 modalities are benchmarked individually against the combined run.
2. **Binary Fixture Portability**:
   - Rather than relying on git LFS or external URLs, [`fixtures_builder.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/app/demo/fixtures_builder.py) dynamically ensures valid QR PNGs, screenshot PNGs, and 16kHz WAV audio files exist in `app/demo/fixtures/` whenever the application initializes.

## 2. n8n Workflow Output Node Design & Rationale

For the output node in [`n8n/workflows/high_severity_alert.json`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/n8n/workflows/high_severity_alert.json), we chose **`Respond to Webhook` (`n8n-nodes-base.respondToWebhook`)** combined with an upstream **JavaScript Code node (`Format Incident Alert`)**.

### Why this design avoids fragility:
1. **Zero External Credentials**: It does not depend on a live Slack bot token, Discord webhook URL, or Google Sheets OAuth credentials, eliminating any chance of rate limits, expired API keys, or network firewall blocks during a viva demonstration.
2. **Synchronous Delivery Confirmation**: The `Respond to Webhook` node sends an immediate `200 OK` JSON response back to ThreatWeave with the formatted incident confirmation, providing instant verification in both ThreatWeave's API logs and n8n's execution history.
3. **Full Observability**: The incident message, severity rating, and extracted indicators are visible directly inside n8n's visual node execution inspect pane.

---

## 3. Proof: Zero Network Calls When Unset (`assert_not_called`)

From [`apps/api/tests/test_webhook.py`](file:///d:/ThreatWeave%20—%20Agentic%20Multimodal/threatweave/apps/api/tests/test_webhook.py#L30-L46):

```python
@patch("httpx.Client")
def test_webhook_url_unset_results_in_zero_network_calls(self, mock_client_cls):
    """When N8N_WEBHOOK_URL is empty, notify_high_severity returns immediately without HTTP calls."""
    with patch.object(settings, "N8N_WEBHOOK_URL", ""):
        payload = {
            "investigation_id": "test-id-123",
            "title": "Test Incident",
            "final_risk_score": 0.95,
            "final_severity": "critical",
            "top_indicators": ["urgency_language"],
        }
        webhook_service.notify_high_severity(payload)

        # Assert absolutely no HTTP client was created or called
        mock_client_cls.assert_not_called()
```
**Result**: Passed (`mock_client_cls.assert_not_called()` confirmed).

## 5. Confirmation: Non-Fatal Graceful Degradation

If the n8n container is completely stopped, times out, or returns a 500 error:
- Unit test `test_webhook_unreachable_does_not_raise` in `test_webhook.py` simulates connection failure.
- Integration test `test_webhook_failure_does_not_fail_investigation` injects a runtime exception.
- In all failure modes, ThreatWeave logs a warning and ensures the investigation cleanly achieves `status="completed"` with all findings and risk breakdowns fully intact.

## 6. Ambiguities Resolved
1. **Severity Threshold Ordering**:
   - Resolved by implementing `SEVERITY_RANKS = {"low": 1, "medium": 2, "high": 3, "critical": 4}` in `orchestration_service.py` to ensure configurable threshold comparison handles case-insensitivity and rank evaluation cleanly.
2. **Top Indicator Aggregation**:
   - Used `collections.Counter` across all swarm findings in `final_state["findings"]` to extract the top 3–5 most prominent unique threat indicators for inclusion in the webhook payload.
